<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a finite three-electron Coulomb molecule whose spin-summed ground-state density cannot be reproduced by any ground-state ensemble of noninteracting electrons in a single real, spin-independent local potential in $`L^{3/2}(\mathbf R^3)+L^\infty(\mathbf R^3)`$. The molecule has two equal positive integer nuclear charges, specified by an exact finite nonnumerical formula.

- **claim-main-noncounterpart** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Set $D=10^{200}$ and $e_z=(0,0,1)$. There is a finite positive integer $Z$, specified by [eq:explicit-charge](#eq:explicit-charge), for which the Coulomb Hamiltonian $$H_C=\sum_{i=1}^3 \left(-\frac12\Delta_i -\frac{Z}{\|r_i-(D/Z)e_z\|} -\frac{Z}{\|r_i+(D/Z)e_z\|}\right) +\sum_{1\le i<j\le3}\frac1{\|r_i-r_j\|}$$ has a normalizable absolute ground state $\Psi$ in the sector $S_z=1/2$, normalized by $\\|\Psi\\|_2=1$, but there is no $v\in\mathcal V$ and no ground-state ensemble $\Gamma$ of $H_s(v)$ such that $$n_\Gamma=n_\Psi\qquad\text{almost everywhere on }\mathbf R^3.$$
  - Explanation: For one explicit finite two-nucleus molecule with three electrons, the interacting ground-state density is not the density of any ground-state ensemble of noninteracting electrons in a single real spin-independent potential from the sum space. The charge is fixed by an exact nonnumerical formula.
- **claim-scaling-dilation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-scaling)): Suppose that the density of a ground state of $H_{3,1/Z}$, for a positive integer $Z$, has no noninteracting ground-state ensemble representation with a real spin-independent potential in $L^{3/2}(\mathbf R^3)+L^\infty(\mathbf R^3)$. The physical Coulomb system with three electrons and charges $Z,Z$ at $$(0,0,D/Z),\qquad (0,0,-D/Z)$$ then has a ground-state density with the same obstruction.
  - Explanation: A unitary dilation moves the obstruction from the scaled problem with repulsion coefficient one over the charge to the physical molecule with integer charges, since the dilated potential stays in the same sum space.
- **claim-two-centre-spectrum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-two-centre-spectrum)): For $D\ge100$, the two lowest eigenvalues of $h_D$ are simple. They have normalized real, axisymmetric eigenfunctions $b,e$, where $b>0$ is even and $e$ is odd and strictly positive on $z>0$. Write $$\epsilon_b=-\frac{\gamma^2}{2},\qquad \epsilon_e=-\frac{k_0^2}{2},\qquad d_*=\epsilon_e-\epsilon_b .$$ Then $$0<d_*<\frac{100}{D},\qquad \|\gamma-1\|+\|k_0-1\|<\frac{200}{D}. \label{eq:two-centre-numerics}$$ The spectrum on $\{b,e\}^{\perp}$ lies more than $0.2$ above $\epsilon_e$. In particular, the spectral gap above $b$ in the even subspace is greater than $0.2$.
  - Explanation: For large separation the two lowest levels of the two-centre one-electron operator are simple with real axisymmetric eigenfunctions, one even and one odd. Their splitting is small and the rest of the spectrum lies a fixed distance higher.
- **claim-small-coupling-ground-states** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-small-coupling)): Fix $D\ge100$. For all sufficiently small $\lambda\ge0$, the ionic operator $H_{2,\lambda}$ has a simple isolated ground state, and $H_{3,\lambda}$ has a simple isolated ground state $\Psi_\lambda$ in the sector $S_z=1/2$. Its energy is an absolute ground energy of $H_{3,\lambda}$.* *The ionic ground state is a spin singlet, is even under simultaneous reflection of its two spatial coordinates, and is axisymmetric. The state $\Psi_\lambda$ is real, axisymmetric, and odd under simultaneous reflection of its three spatial coordinates. The states and energies can be chosen analytic near zero; in particular, their state vectors converge with error $O(\lambda)$ in $H^2$. At zero coupling, $$\Psi_0=b\uparrow\wedge b\downarrow\wedge e\uparrow,\qquad E_2(0)=2\epsilon_b,\qquad E_3(0)=2\epsilon_b+\epsilon_e . \label{eq:zero-coupling-states}$$ The ionic state has spatial factor $b(x_1)b(x_2)$ and the normalized singlet spin factor.
  - Explanation: For small repulsion the two-electron ionic ground state and the three-electron ground state in the sector of spin projection one half are simple, isolated and analytic in the coupling. The three-electron energy is an absolute ground energy and the states keep their symmetries.
- **claim-density-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-density-regularity)): Regard $F_{\rm true}(x)=\sqrt3\,\Psi_\lambda(x,\cdot)$ as a Hilbert-space-valued function, retaining the removed-electron spin and all ionic coordinates and spins. Then $$F_{\rm true}\in H^2(\mathbf R^3;\mathcal K),\qquad n_\lambda=\\|F_{\rm true}\\|_{\mathcal K}^2 \in H^2(\mathbf R^3)\cap L^1(\mathbf R^3),\qquad \sqrt{n_\lambda}\in H^1(\mathbf R^3),$$ where $\mathcal K$ is the corresponding fiber Hilbert space. Moreover $\Delta n_\lambda\in L^1(\mathbf R^3)$. These assertions hold for every $H^2$ three-electron eigenstate, with no small-coupling assumption.* *For the branch in [prop:small-coupling](#prop:small-coupling), $n_\lambda\to n_0=2b^2+e^2$ in $H^2$ and $L^1$, hence uniformly on compact sets; the densities are commonly tight as $\lambda\downarrow0$. Each fixed compact set has a positive lower bound for $n_\lambda$ for sufficiently small $\lambda$. The density is even and axisymmetric.
  - Explanation: The fiber-valued wavefunction and the density have Sobolev regularity for any eigenstate, and along the small-coupling branch the density converges to the zero-coupling density with common tightness and compact positive lower bounds.
- **claim-density-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-density-properties)): For all sufficiently large $D$ and sufficiently small $\lambda\geq0$, the density $n=n_\lambda$ is positive, even in $z$, and axisymmetric. It is smooth away from the nuclei and converges locally uniformly to $2b^2+e^2$ as $\lambda\downarrow0$.* *Given $\eta>0$, first $D$ can be chosen sufficiently large and then $\lambda_D>0$ sufficiently small so that, beyond a radius independent of $0\leq\lambda\leq\lambda_D$, $$\|\partial_r\log n+2k\|\leq\eta,\qquad \left\|\partial_p\log n+2k\frac p r\right\|\leq\eta \quad(0\leq z\leq p/2). \label{eq:density-drift}$$ With $L=10$ and $m(p,z)=e^{2kp}n(p,z)$, the same choices give $$\frac{\|m_p\|+\|m_{pp}\|}{m}\leq\eta,\qquad \frac{n(p,z_1)}{n(p,z_2)}\leq20 \quad(0\leq z_1\leq z_2\leq L) \label{eq:strip-data}$$ for all sufficiently large $p$, uniformly in that coupling interval.* *For each fixed such $\lambda>0$, there are $c_\lambda>0$ and $P_\lambda<\infty$ such that $$\sqrt{n(p,0)}\geq c_\lambda A(p)p^{-4}\qquad(p\geq P_\lambda). \label{eq:plane-tail}$$ There is a number $a>0$, depending on $D,\lambda$, with the following translation properties. If $x_j=p_j\omega_j$ lies in the median plane, $p_j\to\infty$, and the horizontal unit vectors $\omega_j\to\omega_*$, then $$\frac{n(x_j+x)}{(A(p_j)/p_j)^2} \longrightarrow a^2z^2e^{-2k\omega_*\cdot x} \quad\hbox{locally uniformly for }x=(x_1,x_2,z)\in\mathbf R^3. \label{eq:plane-translate}$$ If $\|x_j\|\to\infty$, $\|(x_j)_3\|\to\infty$, and $x_j/\|x_j\|\to\omega_*$, then $$\frac{n(x_j+x)}{n(x_j)} \longrightarrow e^{-2k\omega_*\cdot x} \quad\hbox{locally uniformly in }\mathbf R^3. \label{eq:bulk-translate}$$
  - Explanation: The density is positive, even in the vertical coordinate and axisymmetric, with controlled logarithmic drift, strip bounds, a plane tail lower bound at fixed coupling, and two distinct translation limits near the median plane and away from it.
- **claim-channel-cutoff** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-channel-cutoff)): There are an exterior $\mathcal K$-valued function $F$, a nonnegative bounded multiplication operator $W(x)$ on the ionic variables, and $\sigma>\gamma$ such that $$\left(-\frac{\Delta_x}{2}+\frac{k^2}{2}+B+u(x)+\lambda W(x)\right)F=R. \label{eq:cutoff-channel-equation}$$ The operator $W$ equals $\sum_{i=1}^2\|x-y_i\|^{-1}$ for $\|y\|\leq r/2$, vanishes for $\|y\|\geq3r/4$, and satisfies $$\\|\partial_x^\nu W(x)\\|_{\mathcal K\to\mathcal K} \leq C_\nu r^{-1-\|\nu\|}. \label{eq:cutoff-interaction-symbol}$$ Here $\nu$ is a Cartesian multiindex. For every fixed integer $s$, $$\\|\partial_x^\nu R(x)\\|\leq C_{D,s}\lambda e^{-\sigma r} \quad(\|\nu\|\leq s). \label{eq:channel-residual}$$ Moreover, $$\left\|\partial_x^\nu\{n(x)-\\|F(x)\\|^2\}\right\| \leq C_D e^{-2\sigma r}\quad(\|\nu\|\leq2), \label{eq:density-cutoff-error}$$ and $F-F_0=O(\lambda)$ on each fixed exterior annulus, also in any fixed finite number of $x$-derivatives.
  - Explanation: In the exterior region the fiber function satisfies a cutoff channel equation with a bounded interaction multiplier and a residual that decays faster than the fast core term; the density cutoff error is exponentially small.
- **claim-channel-expansion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-channel-expansion)): Write $PF=\phi f$. With every fixed finite number of angular derivatives, $$\\|F\\|=O(A),\qquad \\|QF\\|=O(A/r^2). \label{eq:channel-leading-bounds}$$ There are smooth real odd scalar functions $a_0,a_1,a_2$ on $\mathbb S^2$ such that $$f=A\left(a_0+\frac{a_1}{r}+\frac{a_2}{r^2}+\mathcal E\right). \label{eq:projected-channel-expansion}$$ For each fixed angular differential operator $\Omega^\ell$, $$\Omega^\ell\mathcal E=O(r^{-3}),\qquad \partial_r\Omega^\ell\mathcal E=O(r^{-4}),\qquad \partial_r^2\Omega^\ell\mathcal E=O(r^{-4}). \label{eq:scalar-remainder-derivatives}$$ The radial remainders are odd in $\omega_z$; in particular their bounds without angular differentiation gain a factor $\|\omega_z\|$, also after one or two radial derivatives. All bounds are uniform for small $\lambda$. Furthermore $a_0^\lambda\to a_0^0$ in $C^1(\mathbb S^2)$, and phases can be chosen so that $$\inf_{\omega_z>0}\frac{a_0^\lambda(\omega)}{\omega_z}>c_D>0. \label{eq:simple-angular-zero}$$ Finally, $$b=e^{-\gamma r}r^{2/\gamma-1} \bigl(b_\infty(\omega)+O(r^{-1})\bigr),\qquad \min_{\mathbb S^2}b_\infty>0, \label{eq:fast-orbital-expansion}$$ with angular derivatives and two radial derivatives of the expansion.
  - Explanation: The ground-ionic projection of the fiber function has a three-term expansion with odd angular coefficients and remainder estimates uniform in small coupling, while the complementary part is smaller by an inverse square of the radius. The leading angular coefficient has a simple zero.
- **claim-plane-coefficient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-plane-coefficient)): There are smooth exponentially localized $Q\mathcal K$-valued angular coefficients $T_2,T_3,T_4$, bounded uniformly for small $\lambda$, such that $$QF=bG+\lambda A \left(\frac{T_2}{r^2}+\frac{T_3}{r^3}+\frac{T_4}{r^4}\right) +\mathcal E_Q,\qquad \\|\partial_x^\nu\mathcal E_Q\\| \leq C_D\lambda Ar^{-5}\quad(\|\nu\|\leq2). \label{eq:complementary-expansion}$$ Writing $Y_z=y_{1z}+y_{2z}$, one has $$\begin{split} T_2&=-a_0B_Q^{-1}QW_2\phi,\\ T_2\|_{\omega_z=0}&=T_3\|_{\omega_z=0}=0,\\ T_4\|_{\omega_z=0} &=\frac12B_Q^{-1}\Delta_{\mathbb S^2}T_2\|_{\omega_z=0} =-a_0'(0)B_Q^{-2}QY_z\phi . \end{split} \label{eq:equatorial-coefficient}$$ The last vector has norm bounded below by a positive constant at fixed $D$, uniformly for sufficiently small $\lambda$. All $T_j$ have distinguished spin up.
  - Explanation: The complementary channel has a multipole expansion whose first nonzero term on the median plane is the fourth-order coefficient, which is nonzero. This supplies a density tail on the plane with the same exponential rate.
- **claim-fixed-parameter-channels** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-fixed-parameter-channels)): Suppose $D$ satisfies [prop:two-centre-spectrum](#prop:two-centre-spectrum) and $$k_0>\sqrt{0.98},\qquad \gamma<\sqrt{49/45}.$$ Fix $t\in(0,1]$. Assume the two-electron ground level is isolated and simple with an even ionic singlet, and the three-electron ground level in $S_z=1/2$ is isolated and simple, is an absolute ground level, and has a real axisymmetric odd ground state. Assume $n_t>0$, and set $$k=\sqrt{2(E_2(t)-E_3(t))},\qquad A_t(r)=e^{-kr}r^{(2-2t)/k-1}.$$ Suppose $$\begin{gathered} 2\epsilon_b-E_3(t)>0.49,\qquad 0.99<k<\gamma,\\ \inf_{\omega_z>0}\liminf_{r\to\infty} \frac{\\|P_tF_{\mathrm{true},t}(r\omega)\\|} {\omega_z A_t(r)}>0. \end{gathered} \label{eq:fixed-channel-hypotheses}$$ Then $n_t$ has the local regularity in [lem:density-regularity](#lem:density-regularity), is smooth away from the nuclei, and satisfies [eq:plane-tail](#eq:plane-tail), [eq:plane-translate](#eq:plane-translate), [eq:bulk-translate](#eq:bulk-translate) at this fixed coupling, with $A=A_t$ and suitable positive constants. Membership of $t$ in the preceding perturbative interval is not required.
  - Explanation: At a fixed positive coupling, spectral and amplitude hypotheses suffice for the density regularity, the plane lower tail and both translation limits, without membership in the earlier perturbative interval.
- **claim-angle-minimizer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-angle-minimizer)): Let $n$ be a positive, continuous, even density with $\int n=3$, $n\in H^2_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbf R^3)$, and $\sqrt n\in H^1(\mathbf R^3)$. The problem $$\label{eq:angle-minimum} \begin{aligned} &\min_{\vartheta\in H^1(n;\mathbf R)} \int_{\mathbf R^3}n\|\nabla\vartheta\|^2,\\ &\text{subject to }\quad \vartheta\text{ odd},\qquad \int_{\mathbf R^3}n\sin^2\vartheta=1 \end{aligned}$$ has a minimizer $\theta$ satisfying $0\le\theta\le\pi/2$ on $\Omega_+$. Every such folded minimizer has a multiplier $d>0$ and satisfies $$\label{eq:angle-euler} L_n\theta:=n^{-1}\operatorname{div}(n\nabla\theta) =-d\sin(2\theta) \quad\hbox{on }\mathbf R^3.$$ It belongs locally to $W^{2,p}$ for every $p<6$, and $$0<\theta<\pi/2\quad\hbox{on }\Omega_+.$$ In particular, it is locally $C^{1,\alpha}$ for every $0<\alpha<1/2$. Axial symmetry of $n$ is not required for this proposition, and axial symmetry of $\theta$ is not asserted.
  - Explanation: The constrained odd angle problem has a folded minimizer with a positive multiplier, satisfying a divergence-form Euler equation with local Sobolev regularity and strict bounds above the median plane.
- **claim-angle-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-angle-convergence)): For every choice of folded minimizing angles for $n_\lambda$, $$\label{eq:angle-convergence} \theta_\lambda\longrightarrow \theta_*=\arctan\!\left(\frac{e}{\sqrt2\,b}\right), \qquad d_\lambda\longrightarrow d_*=\epsilon_e-\epsilon_b.$$ The angle convergence is weak in local $H^1$, strong in local $L^2$, and in $C^1$ on every fixed compact set. In particular, all these conclusions hold uniformly over the set of folded minimizers in the sequential sense.
  - Explanation: As the coupling tends to zero every folded minimizing angle converges to the zero-coupling reference angle and the multiplier converges to the orbital energy splitting, in the stated local topologies.
- **claim-orbital-equations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-orbital-equations)): For a folded minimizer define $$\label{eq:angle-orbitals} g=\sqrt n\cos\theta,\qquad h=\sqrt n\sin\theta.$$ Then $g,h\in H^1(\mathbf R^3)\cap H^2_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbf R^3)$, $g$ is even and strictly positive, and $h$ is odd and strictly positive on $\Omega_+$. Moreover, $$\\|g\\|_2^2=2,\qquad \\|h\\|_2^2=1,\qquad \langle g,h\rangle=0,\qquad g^2+h^2=n.$$ The real function $$\label{eq:formal-potential} V=\frac{\Delta g}{2g} =\frac{\Delta\sqrt n}{2\sqrt n} -\frac{\|\nabla\theta\|^2}{2}+d\sin^2\theta$$ belongs to $L^2_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbf R^3)$, and $$\label{eq:orbital-equations} (-\Delta/2+V)g=0,\qquad (-\Delta/2+V)h=dh$$ in distributions. On the median plane, $g=\sqrt n$ and $h=0$.
  - Explanation: The angle splits the density into two orbitals with prescribed norms satisfying one common formal potential equation at two different energies; the potential is locally square integrable.
- **claim-strip-solution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-strip-solution)): Suppose, for $p\ge P_0$ and $0\le z\le L$, that $m(p,z)=e^{2kp}n(p,z)>0$ satisfies $$\label{eq:strip-comparison-data} \begin{aligned} \|m_p\|+\|m_{pp}\|&\le\eta m,\qquad 0<\eta\le1,\\ \frac{m(p,t)}{m(p,s)}&\le C \qquad(0\le t\le s\le L). \end{aligned}$$ Let $U(p,\cdot)$ solve $$\label{eq:strip-ode} n^{-1}(nU_z)_z=\mu^2U,\qquad U(p,0)=1,\quad U(p,L)=0.$$ It is positive and decreasing on $0\le z<L$, and $$\label{eq:strip-horizontal-derivatives} \|U_p\|+\|U_{pp}\|\le C_2\eta U,$$ where $C_2$ depends only on $C,L,\mu$. When $C\le100$, one may take $C_2<10^{30}$.
  - Explanation: Under bounded drift and ratio hypotheses the one-dimensional strip problem has a positive decreasing solution whose horizontal derivatives are bounded by a constant times the drift size.
- **claim-exterior-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-exterior-gap)): For sufficiently large $D$, there are $\lambda_0>0$ and $R_0<\infty$ such that every folded minimizing angle for $0\le\lambda<\lambda_0$ satisfies $$\label{eq:exterior-weighted-gap} \frac12\int_{\Omega_+}g^2\|\nabla\xi\|^2 \ge0.03\int_{\Omega_+}g^2\|\xi\|^2$$ for tests supported in $\{\|x\|>R_0\}$. No zero trace on $z=0$ is required. The inequality holds throughout the corresponding weighted form domain.
  - Explanation: Far from the nuclei the weighted gradient form of the orbital weight has a uniform positive lower bound for every folded minimizer, with no zero trace on the median plane needed.
- **claim-weighted-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-weighted-gap)): For sufficiently large $D$ and all sufficiently small $\lambda\ge0$, every folded minimizing angle satisfies $$\label{eq:weighted-gap} \frac12\int_{\mathbf R^3}g^2\|\nabla\xi\|^2 \ge d\left( \int_{\mathbf R^3}g^2\|\xi\|^2 -\frac{\left\|\int_{\mathbf R^3}g^2\xi\right\|^2} {\int_{\mathbf R^3}g^2}\right), \qquad \xi\in H^1(g^2).$$
  - Explanation: The full weighted form has a spectral gap at the second orbital bounded below by the multiplier, combining an odd ground-state transform with an even-sector compactness argument controlled by the exterior gap.
- **claim-quantitative-gap** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-quantitative-gap)): Let $b,e,n_0,\theta_*$ be the zero-coupling data for a fixed $D$ whose even gap satisfies $\Delta_{\mathrm{ev}}>0.2$. Let $n$ satisfy the hypotheses of [prop:angle-minimizer](#prop:angle-minimizer), be axisymmetric, and let $\theta$ be a folded minimizer. Set $$\eta_0=10^{-60},\qquad M\ge10^4,\qquad S=100M.$$ Suppose $n$ is $C^2$ on a neighborhood of $\{r\ge M\}$, $0.99<k<1.01$, $0<d<0.001$, and the following tests hold.* 1. *The two drift bounds of [eq:density-drift](#eq:density-drift) hold with $\eta=\eta_0$ for $r\ge M$ on their stated domains. For $p\ge M,\ 0\le z\le L$, the bounds [eq:strip-comparison-data](#eq:strip-comparison-data) hold with $\eta=\eta_0$ and $C=100$.* 2. *Let $U,U_0$ be the strip solutions for $n,n_0$, and suppose $$\alpha=\inf_{0<z\le L} \frac{(2/\pi)\delta_*(M,z)-U_0(M,z)}{z}>0, \qquad \delta_*=\pi/2-\theta_*.$$ Uniformly on the cylinder $p=M,\ 0\le z\le L$, including all azimuths for $\theta$, one has $$\|\partial_z(\theta-\theta_*)\| +\|\partial_z(U-U_0)\|<\alpha/2.$$* 3. *On $r\le2S$, the local weight comparison is $$\frac12\le\frac{g^2}{2b^2}\le2.$$* *Then holds for tests supported in $r>S$, and the full gap [eq:weighted-gap](#eq:weighted-gap) holds.
  - Explanation: A finite list of drift, strip, inner-boundary and weight-comparison tests at one coupling implies the exterior gap beyond a fixed radius and the full weighted gap, with no perturbative compactness.
- **claim-ensemble-certificate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-ensemble-certificate)): Assume the density regularity of [lem:density-regularity](#lem:density-regularity), the orbital hypotheses of [prop:orbital-equations](#prop:orbital-equations), and the weighted gap [eq:weighted-gap](#eq:weighted-gap). Then the formal potential $V$ satisfies $\int_{\mathbf R^3}n\|V\|<\infty$. Every ensemble with density $n$ and finite kinetic energy obeys $$T[\Gamma]+\int_{\mathbf R^3}Vn\ge d. \label{eq:ensemble-certificate}$$ The determinant $\Phi_\theta$ attains equality. Consequently, $$T_{\mathrm{ens}}[n] =T[\|\Phi_\theta\rangle\langle\Phi_\theta\|] =d-\int_{\mathbf R^3}Vn. \label{eq:ensemble-minimum}$$
  - Explanation: Under the regularity, orbital and gap hypotheses, every finite-kinetic-energy ensemble with the given density obeys a lower bound that the paired determinant attains, so the determinant minimizes the ensemble constrained search.
- **claim-potential-identification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-potential-identification)): Assume the hypotheses of [prop:ensemble-certificate](#prop:ensemble-certificate). Let $v\in L^{3/2}(\mathbf R^3)+L^\infty(\mathbf R^3)$ be real and spin independent, and let $h_v=-\Delta/2+v$ be its spatial one-particle operator, defined by the closed form on $H^1(\mathbf R^3)$. Suppose $H_v=\sum_{i=1}^3(h_v\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_{\mathbf C^2})_i$ on $\mathfrak H_3$ has a ground eigenspace supporting an ensemble $\Gamma_s$ with $n_{\Gamma_s}=n$. Then $\Phi_\theta$ belongs to that ground eigenspace, and there is a real constant $e_g$ such that $$h_v g=e_g g, \qquad v=V+e_g\quad\text{almost everywhere}. \label{eq:ensemble-potential-identification}$$ In particular, $V\in L^{3/2}(\mathbf R^3)+L^\infty(\mathbf R^3)$.
  - Explanation: Any admissible potential whose noninteracting ground space supports an ensemble with this density must coincide almost everywhere with the formal potential up to an additive constant, and the determinant lies in that ground space.
- **claim-translated-orbital-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-translated-orbital-compactness)): Write $V=V_{\mathrm c}+V_{\mathrm b}$, where $V_{\mathrm c}\in L^{3/2}(\mathbf R^3)$ and $V_{\mathrm b}\in L^\infty(\mathbf R^3)$. If $\|y_j\|\to\infty$, then $$V_{\mathrm c}(y_j+\cdot)\longrightarrow0 \quad\text{in }L^{3/2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbf R^3).$$ After passing to a subsequence there is a real bounded function $W$ such that $$V_{\mathrm b}(y_j+\cdot)\stackrel{*}{\rightharpoonup}W \quad\text{locally in }L^\infty, \qquad \\|W\\|_\infty\le\\|V_{\mathrm b}\\|_\infty.$$ Suppose, in addition, that $u_j\in H^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$ solves $$\left(-\frac12\Delta+V(y_j+\cdot)\right)u_j=\varepsilon u_j$$ for one fixed real $\varepsilon$, and that $u_j$ is bounded in $L^2$ on every compact set. A further subsequence converges strongly in $L^2_{\mathrm{loc}}$ and weakly in $H^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$ to a solution of $$\left(-\frac12\Delta+W\right)u=\varepsilon u.$$
  - Explanation: Along escaping translations the integrable part of the potential vanishes locally while the bounded part has weak-star limits, and solutions of the translated eigenvalue equation subconverge to solutions with the limiting bounded potential.
- **claim-potential-limits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-potential-limits)): Assume [eq:admissible-formal-potential](#eq:admissible-formal-potential) and the density translation limits [eq:plane-translate](#eq:plane-translate), [eq:bulk-translate](#eq:bulk-translate). For every sequence $\|y_j\|\to\infty$, every local weak-star subsequential limit of $V_{\mathrm b}(y_j+\cdot)$ is the constant $$c_\infty=d+\frac{k^2}{2}. \label{eq:potential-limit-constant}$$
  - Explanation: If the formal potential lay in the sum space, every weak-star limit of its translated bounded part along escaping sequences would be one constant determined by the multiplier and the decay rate.
- **claim-exterior-form-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-exterior-form-limit)): Let $V=V_{\mathrm c}+V_{\mathrm b}$ be as in [lem:translated-orbital-compactness](#lem:translated-orbital-compactness). Suppose every bounded-potential limit along escaping translates is a fixed constant $c_\infty$. For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $R_\varepsilon<\infty$ such that $$q_V[u]\ge \left(\frac12-\varepsilon\right)\\|\nabla u\\|_2^2 +(c_\infty-\varepsilon)\\|u\\|_2^2 \label{eq:exterior-form-coercivity}$$ for every $u\in H^1(\mathbf R^3)$ vanishing almost everywhere on $\{\|x\|<R_\varepsilon\}$.
  - Explanation: When all translated bounded-potential limits equal one constant, the potential form is coercive outside a large ball with the gradient and mass coefficients close to the free ones.
- **claim-fixed-density-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-obstruction)): Let $n,\theta,g,h,V,d$ have the analytic properties stated at the beginning of this section, with $d>0$. Suppose that [eq:plane-tail](#eq:plane-tail), [eq:plane-translate](#eq:plane-translate), [eq:bulk-translate](#eq:bulk-translate) hold with fixed $k>0$ and $\beta$, and that the weighted gap [eq:weighted-gap](#eq:weighted-gap) holds. Then $$V% \BeginAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct,method=hex,unicode,ActualText=2209}% \KSOriginalNotin\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}L^{3/2}(\mathbf R^3)+L^\infty(\mathbf R^3).$$ Moreover, $n$ is not the density of a ground-state ensemble of any three-electron noninteracting Hamiltonian with a single real, spin-independent potential in this class. These hypotheses are all fixed-parameter hypotheses; no perturbative neighborhood is required.
  - Explanation: With the analytic properties, the plane asymptotics and the weighted gap, the formal potential is not in the sum space, so the density is not that of a ground-state ensemble of any noninteracting Hamiltonian with such a potential. An exponentially weighted trace on the median plane gives the contradiction.
- **claim-qualitative-counterexample** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-thm-qualitative-counterexample)): For every sufficiently large fixed $D$, there is an integer $Z_0(D)$ such that, for every integer $Z\ge Z_0(D)$, the three-electron Coulomb Hamiltonian with nuclear charges $Z,Z$ at $(0,0,\pm D/Z)$ has an absolute ground state whose density is not representable by a ground-state ensemble of a single real, spin-independent noninteracting potential in $L^{3/2}(\mathbf R^3)+L^\infty(\mathbf R^3)$.
  - Explanation: For every sufficiently large separation parameter there are integer charges beyond a threshold for which the physical molecule has a ground-state density without a noninteracting ensemble representation in the sum space.
- **claim-parameter-radius** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-parameter-radius)): The set in [eq:parameter-radius](#eq:parameter-radius) is nonempty. Consequently $M$ is a finite integer, and the number $\alpha$ in [eq:parameter-alpha](#eq:parameter-alpha) is strictly positive.
  - Explanation: The defining set for the comparison radius is nonempty, so the radius is a finite integer and the inner boundary margin is strictly positive.
- **claim-explicit-charge-finite** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-explicit-charge-finite)): The supremum in [eq:explicit-charge](#eq:explicit-charge) is finite. The resulting $Z$ is a positive integer, and $t_*=1/Z$ is regular and lies outside $\mathcal B$.
  - Explanation: The supremum defining the charge is finite because small couplings avoid the bad set, so the charge is a positive integer and its reciprocal is regular and outside the bad set.
- **claim-hxc-first-variation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-hxc-first-variation)): For the physical ground-state density $n=n_\Psi$ of [thm:main](#thm:main), $\partial_{\mathcal V}T_{\mathrm{ens}}[n]=\varnothing$. Moreover, there is no $w\in\mathcal V$ such that, for every $m\in\mathcal D$, $$\lim_{t\downarrow0} \frac{E_{\mathrm{Hxc}}[n+t(m-n)]-E_{\mathrm{Hxc}}[n]}{t} =\int_{\mathbf R^3}w(m-n). \label{eq:hxc-full-segment-variation}$$
  - Explanation: At the physical density the potential-class subdifferential of the ensemble kinetic search is empty, and no potential gives the one-sided variation of the Hartree-exchange-correlation functional along every segment in the finite-energy domain.

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-small-coupling",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $D\\ge100$. For all sufficiently small $\\lambda\\ge0$, the ionic operator $H_{2,\\lambda}$ has a simple isolated ground state, and $H_{3,\\lambda}$ has a simple isolated ground state $\\Psi_\\lambda$ in the sector $S_z=1/2$. Its energy is an absolute ground energy of $H_{3,\\lambda}$.*\n\n*The ionic ground state is a spin singlet, is even under simultaneous reflection of its two spatial coordinates, and is axisymmetric. The state $\\Psi_\\lambda$ is real, axisymmetric, and odd under simultaneous reflection of its three spatial coordinates. The states and energies can be chosen analytic near zero; in particular, their state vectors converge with error $O(\\lambda)$ in $H^2$. At zero coupling, $$\\Psi_0=b\\uparrow\\wedge b\\downarrow\\wedge e\\uparrow,\\qquad\n E_2(0)=2\\epsilon_b,\\qquad\n E_3(0)=2\\epsilon_b+\\epsilon_e .\n \\label{eq:zero-coupling-states}$$ The ionic state has spatial factor $b(x_1)b(x_2)$ and the normalized singlet spin factor.",
        "source_id": "PM-278.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-small-coupling",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $D\\ge100$. Its energy is an absolute ground energy of $H_{3,\\lambda}$.*\n\n*The ionic ground state is a spin singlet, is even under simultaneous reflection of its two spatial coordinates, and is axisymmetric. The state $\\Psi_\\lambda$ is real, axisymmetric, and odd under simultaneous reflection of its three spatial coordinates. The states and energies can be chosen analytic near zero; in particular, their state vectors converge with error $O(\\lambda)$ in $H^2$. At zero coupling, $$\\Psi_0=b\\uparrow\\wedge b\\downarrow\\wedge e\\uparrow,\\qquad\n E_2(0)=2\\epsilon_b,\\qquad\n E_3(0)=2\\epsilon_b+\\epsilon_e .\n \\label{eq:zero-coupling-states}$$ The ionic state has spatial factor $b(x_1)b(x_2)$ and the normalized singlet spin factor.",
          "source_id": "PM-278.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix $D\\ge100$. Its energy is an absolute ground energy of $H_{3,\\lambda}$.*\n\n*The ionic ground state is a spin singlet, is even under simultaneous reflection of its two spatial coordinates, and is axisymmetric. The state $\\Psi_\\lambda$ is real, axisymmetric, and odd under simultaneous reflection of its three spatial coordinates. The states and energies can be chosen analytic near zero; in particular, their state vectors converge with error $O(\\lambda)$ in $H^2$. At zero coupling, $$\\Psi_0=b\\uparrow\\wedge b\\downarrow\\wedge e\\uparrow,\\qquad\n E_2(0)=2\\epsilon_b,\\qquad\n E_3(0)=2\\epsilon_b+\\epsilon_e .\n \\label{eq:zero-coupling-states}$$ The ionic state has spatial factor $b(x_1)b(x_2)$ and the normalized singlet spin factor.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-density-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "Regard $F_{\\rm true}(x)=\\sqrt3\\,\\Psi_\\lambda(x,\\cdot)$ as a Hilbert-space-valued function, retaining the removed-electron spin and all ionic coordinates and spins. Then $$F_{\\rm true}\\in H^2(\\mathbf R^3;\\mathcal K),\\qquad\n n_\\lambda=\\|F_{\\rm true}\\|_{\\mathcal K}^2\n       \\in H^2(\\mathbf R^3)\\cap L^1(\\mathbf R^3),\\qquad\n \\sqrt{n_\\lambda}\\in H^1(\\mathbf R^3),$$ where $\\mathcal K$ is the corresponding fiber Hilbert space. Moreover $\\Delta n_\\lambda\\in L^1(\\mathbf R^3)$. These assertions hold for every $H^2$ three-electron eigenstate, with no small-coupling assumption.*\n\n*For the branch in [prop:small-coupling](#prop:small-coupling), $n_\\lambda\\to n_0=2b^2+e^2$ in $H^2$ and $L^1$, hence uniformly on compact sets; the densities are commonly tight as $\\lambda\\downarrow0$. Each fixed compact set has a positive lower bound for $n_\\lambda$ for sufficiently small $\\lambda$. The density is even and axisymmetric.",
        "source_id": "PM-278.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-density-regularity",
          "exact_quote": "Regard $F_{\\rm true}(x)=\\sqrt3\\,\\Psi_\\lambda(x,\\cdot)$ as a Hilbert-space-valued function, retaining the removed-electron spin and all ionic coordinates and spins. Moreover $\\Delta n_\\lambda\\in L^1(\\mathbf R^3)$. These assertions hold for every $H^2$ three-electron eigenstate, with no small-coupling assumption.*\n\n*For the branch in [prop:small-coupling](#prop:small-coupling), $n_\\lambda\\to n_0=2b^2+e^2$ in $H^2$ and $L^1$, hence uniformly on compact sets; the densities are commonly tight as $\\lambda\\downarrow0$. Each fixed compact set has a positive lower bound for $n_\\lambda$ for sufficiently small $\\lambda$. The density is even and axisymmetric.",
          "source_id": "PM-278.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Regard $F_{\\rm true}(x)=\\sqrt3\\,\\Psi_\\lambda(x,\\cdot)$ as a Hilbert-space-valued function, retaining the removed-electron spin and all ionic coordinates and spins.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-density-properties",
        "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $D$ and sufficiently small $\\lambda\\geq0$, the density $n=n_\\lambda$ is positive, even in $z$, and axisymmetric. It is smooth away from the nuclei and converges locally uniformly to $2b^2+e^2$ as $\\lambda\\downarrow0$.*\n\n*Given $\\eta>0$, first $D$ can be chosen sufficiently large and then $\\lambda_D>0$ sufficiently small so that, beyond a radius independent of $0\\leq\\lambda\\leq\\lambda_D$, $$|\\partial_r\\log n+2k|\\leq\\eta,\\qquad\n \\left|\\partial_p\\log n+2k\\frac p r\\right|\\leq\\eta\n \\quad(0\\leq z\\leq p/2).\n \\label{eq:density-drift}$$ With $L=10$ and $m(p,z)=e^{2kp}n(p,z)$, the same choices give $$\\frac{|m_p|+|m_{pp}|}{m}\\leq\\eta,\\qquad\n \\frac{n(p,z_1)}{n(p,z_2)}\\leq20\n \\quad(0\\leq z_1\\leq z_2\\leq L)\n \\label{eq:strip-data}$$ for all sufficiently large $p$, uniformly in that coupling interval.*\n\n*For each fixed such $\\lambda>0$, there are $c_\\lambda>0$ and $P_\\lambda<\\infty$ such that $$\\sqrt{n(p,0)}\\geq c_\\lambda A(p)p^{-4}\\qquad(p\\geq P_\\lambda).\n \\label{eq:plane-tail}$$ There is a number $a>0$, depending on $D,\\lambda$, with the following translation properties. If $x_j=p_j\\omega_j$ lies in the median plane, $p_j\\to\\infty$, and the horizontal unit vectors $\\omega_j\\to\\omega_*$, then $$\\frac{n(x_j+x)}{(A(p_j)/p_j)^2}\n \\longrightarrow a^2z^2e^{-2k\\omega_*\\cdot x}\n \\quad\\hbox{locally uniformly for }x=(x_1,x_2,z)\\in\\mathbf R^3.\n \\label{eq:plane-translate}$$ If $|x_j|\\to\\infty$, $|(x_j)_3|\\to\\infty$, and $x_j/|x_j|\\to\\omega_*$, then $$\\frac{n(x_j+x)}{n(x_j)}\n \\longrightarrow e^{-2k\\omega_*\\cdot x}\n \\quad\\hbox{locally uniformly in }\\mathbf R^3.\n \\label{eq:bulk-translate}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-278.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-density-properties",
          "exact_quote": "For all sufficiently large $D$ and sufficiently small $\\lambda\\geq0$, the density $n=n_\\lambda$ is positive, even in $z$, and axisymmetric. It is smooth away from the nuclei and converges locally uniformly to $2b^2+e^2$ as $\\lambda\\downarrow0$.*\n\n*Given $\\eta>0$, first $D$ can be chosen sufficiently large and then $\\lambda_D>0$ sufficiently small so that, beyond a radius independent of $0\\leq\\lambda\\leq\\lambda_D$, $$|\\partial_r\\log n+2k|\\leq\\eta,\\qquad\n \\left|\\partial_p\\log n+2k\\frac p r\\right|\\leq\\eta\n \\quad(0\\leq z\\leq p/2).\n \\label{eq:density-drift}$$ With $L=10$ and $m(p,z)=e^{2kp}n(p,z)$, the same choices give $$\\frac{|m_p|+|m_{pp}|}{m}\\leq\\eta,\\qquad\n \\frac{n(p,z_1)}{n(p,z_2)}\\leq20\n \\quad(0\\leq z_1\\leq z_2\\leq L)\n \\label{eq:strip-data}$$ for all sufficiently large $p$, uniformly in that coupling interval.*\n\n*For each fixed such $\\lambda>0$, there are $c_\\lambda>0$ and $P_\\lambda<\\infty$ such that $$\\sqrt{n(p,0)}\\geq c_\\lambda A(p)p^{-4}\\qquad(p\\geq P_\\lambda).\n \\label{eq:plane-tail}$$ There is a number $a>0$, depending on $D,\\lambda$, with the following translation properties. If $x_j=p_j\\omega_j$ lies in the median plane, $p_j\\to\\infty$, and the horizontal unit vectors $\\omega_j\\to\\omega_*$, then $$\\frac{n(x_j+x)}{(A(p_j)/p_j)^2}\n \\longrightarrow a^2z^2e^{-2k\\omega_*\\cdot x}\n \\quad\\hbox{locally uniformly for }x=(x_1,x_2,z)\\in\\mathbf R^3.\n \\label{eq:plane-translate}$$ If $|x_j|\\to\\infty$, $|(x_j)_3|\\to\\infty$, and $x_j/|x_j|\\to\\omega_*$, then $$\\frac{n(x_j+x)}{n(x_j)}\n \\longrightarrow e^{-2k\\omega_*\\cdot x}\n \\quad\\hbox{locally uniformly in }\\mathbf R^3.\n \\label{eq:bulk-translate}$$",

      "exact_statement": "For all sufficiently large $D$ and sufficiently small $\\lambda\\geq0$, the density $n=n_\\lambda$ is positive, even in $z$, and axisymmetric. If $x_j=p_j\\omega_j$ lies in the median plane, $p_j\\to\\infty$, and the horizontal unit vectors $\\omega_j\\to\\omega_*$, then $$\\frac{n(x_j+x)}{(A(p_j)/p_j)^2}\n \\longrightarrow a^2z^2e^{-2k\\omega_*\\cdot x}\n \\quad\\hbox{locally uniformly for }x=(x_1,x_2,z)\\in\\mathbf R^3.\n \\label{eq:plane-translate}$$ If $|x_j|\\to\\infty$, $|(x_j)_3|\\to\\infty$, and $x_j/|x_j|\\to\\omega_*$, then $$\\frac{n(x_j+x)}{n(x_j)}\n \\longrightarrow e^{-2k\\omega_*\\cdot x}\n \\quad\\hbox{locally uniformly in }\\mathbf R^3.\n \\label{eq:bulk-translate}$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-channel-cutoff",
        "exact_quote": "There are an exterior $\\mathcal K$-valued function $F$, a nonnegative bounded multiplication operator $W(x)$ on the ionic variables, and $\\sigma>\\gamma$ such that $$\\left(-\\frac{\\Delta_x}{2}+\\frac{k^2}{2}+B+u(x)+\\lambda W(x)\\right)F=R.\n \\label{eq:cutoff-channel-equation}$$ The operator $W$ equals $\\sum_{i=1}^2|x-y_i|^{-1}$ for $|y|\\leq r/2$, vanishes for $|y|\\geq3r/4$, and satisfies $$\\|\\partial_x^\\nu W(x)\\|_{\\mathcal K\\to\\mathcal K}\n \\leq C_\\nu r^{-1-|\\nu|}.\n \\label{eq:cutoff-interaction-symbol}$$ Here $\\nu$ is a Cartesian multiindex. For every fixed integer $s$, $$\\|\\partial_x^\\nu R(x)\\|\\leq C_{D,s}\\lambda e^{-\\sigma r}\n \\quad(|\\nu|\\leq s).\n \\label{eq:channel-residual}$$ Moreover, $$\\left|\\partial_x^\\nu\\{n(x)-\\|F(x)\\|^2\\}\\right|\n \\leq C_D e^{-2\\sigma r}\\quad(|\\nu|\\leq2),\n \\label{eq:density-cutoff-error}$$ and $F-F_0=O(\\lambda)$ on each fixed exterior annulus, also in any fixed finite number of $x$-derivatives.",
        "source_id": "PM-278.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-channel-cutoff",
          "exact_quote": "There are an exterior $\\mathcal K$-valued function $F$, a nonnegative bounded multiplication operator $W(x)$ on the ionic variables, and $\\sigma>\\gamma$ such that $$\\left(-\\frac{\\Delta_x}{2}+\\frac{k^2}{2}+B+u(x)+\\lambda W(x)\\right)F=R.\n \\label{eq:cutoff-channel-equation}$$ The operator $W$ equals $\\sum_{i=1}^2|x-y_i|^{-1}$ for $|y|\\leq r/2$, vanishes for $|y|\\geq3r/4$, and satisfies $$\\|\\partial_x^\\nu W(x)\\|_{\\mathcal K\\to\\mathcal K}\n \\leq C_\\nu r^{-1-|\\nu|}.\n \\label{eq:cutoff-interaction-symbol}$$ Here $\\nu$ is a Cartesian multiindex. For every fixed integer $s$, $$\\|\\partial_x^\\nu R(x)\\|\\leq C_{D,s}\\lambda e^{-\\sigma r}\n \\quad(|\\nu|\\leq s).\n \\label{eq:channel-residual}$$ Moreover, $$\\left|\\partial_x^\\nu\\{n(x)-\\|F(x)\\|^2\\}\\right|\n \\leq C_D e^{-2\\sigma r}\\quad(|\\nu|\\leq2),\n \\label{eq:density-cutoff-error}$$ and $F-F_0=O(\\lambda)$ on each fixed exterior annulus, also in any fixed finite number of $x$-derivatives.",
          "source_id": "PM-278.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are an exterior $\\mathcal K$-valued function $F$, a nonnegative bounded multiplication operator $W(x)$ on the ionic variables, and $\\sigma>\\gamma$ such that $$\\left(-\\frac{\\Delta_x}{2}+\\frac{k^2}{2}+B+u(x)+\\lambda W(x)\\right)F=R.\n \\label{eq:cutoff-channel-equation}$$ The operator $W$ equals $\\sum_{i=1}^2|x-y_i|^{-1}$ for $|y|\\leq r/2$, vanishes for $|y|\\geq3r/4$, and satisfies $$\\|\\partial_x^\\nu W(x)\\|_{\\mathcal K\\to\\mathcal K}\n \\leq C_\\nu r^{-1-|\\nu|}.\n \\label{eq:cutoff-interaction-symbol}$$ Here $\\nu$ is a Cartesian multiindex. For every fixed integer $s$, $$\\|\\partial_x^\\nu R(x)\\|\\leq C_{D,s}\\lambda e^{-\\sigma r}\n \\quad(|\\nu|\\leq s).\n \\label{eq:channel-residual}$$ Moreover, $$\\left|\\partial_x^\\nu\\{n(x)-\\|F(x)\\|^2\\}\\right|\n \\leq C_D e^{-2\\sigma r}\\quad(|\\nu|\\leq2),\n \\label{eq:density-cutoff-error}$$ and $F-F_0=O(\\lambda)$ on each fixed exterior annulus, also in any fixed finite number of $x$-derivatives.",
      "explanation": "In the exterior region the fiber function satisfies a cutoff channel equation with a bounded interaction multiplier and a residual that decays faster than the fast core term; the density cutoff error is exponentially small.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

      "quantifiers": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-channel-expansion",
        "exact_quote": "Write $PF=\\phi f$. With every fixed finite number of angular derivatives, $$\\|F\\|=O(A),\\qquad \\|QF\\|=O(A/r^2).\n \\label{eq:channel-leading-bounds}$$ There are smooth real odd scalar functions $a_0,a_1,a_2$ on $\\mathbb S^2$ such that $$f=A\\left(a_0+\\frac{a_1}{r}+\\frac{a_2}{r^2}+\\mathcal E\\right).\n \\label{eq:projected-channel-expansion}$$ For each fixed angular differential operator $\\Omega^\\ell$, $$\\Omega^\\ell\\mathcal E=O(r^{-3}),\\qquad\n \\partial_r\\Omega^\\ell\\mathcal E=O(r^{-4}),\\qquad\n \\partial_r^2\\Omega^\\ell\\mathcal E=O(r^{-4}).\n \\label{eq:scalar-remainder-derivatives}$$ The radial remainders are odd in $\\omega_z$; in particular their bounds without angular differentiation gain a factor $|\\omega_z|$, also after one or two radial derivatives. All bounds are uniform for small $\\lambda$. Furthermore $a_0^\\lambda\\to a_0^0$ in $C^1(\\mathbb S^2)$, and phases can be chosen so that $$\\inf_{\\omega_z>0}\\frac{a_0^\\lambda(\\omega)}{\\omega_z}>c_D>0.\n \\label{eq:simple-angular-zero}$$ Finally, $$b=e^{-\\gamma r}r^{2/\\gamma-1}\n       \\bigl(b_\\infty(\\omega)+O(r^{-1})\\bigr),\\qquad\n \\min_{\\mathbb S^2}b_\\infty>0,\n \\label{eq:fast-orbital-expansion}$$ with angular derivatives and two radial derivatives of the expansion.",
        "source_id": "PM-278.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-channel-expansion",
          "exact_quote": "Write $PF=\\phi f$. All bounds are uniform for small $\\lambda$. Furthermore $a_0^\\lambda\\to a_0^0$ in $C^1(\\mathbb S^2)$, and phases can be chosen so that $$\\inf_{\\omega_z>0}\\frac{a_0^\\lambda(\\omega)}{\\omega_z}>c_D>0.\n \\label{eq:simple-angular-zero}$$ Finally, $$b=e^{-\\gamma r}r^{2/\\gamma-1}\n       \\bigl(b_\\infty(\\omega)+O(r^{-1})\\bigr),\\qquad\n \\min_{\\mathbb S^2}b_\\infty>0,\n \\label{eq:fast-orbital-expansion}$$ with angular derivatives and two radial derivatives of the expansion.",
          "source_id": "PM-278.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Write $PF=\\phi f$. With every fixed finite number of angular derivatives, $$\\|F\\|=O(A),\\qquad \\|QF\\|=O(A/r^2).\n \\label{eq:channel-leading-bounds}$$ There are smooth real odd scalar functions $a_0,a_1,a_2$ on $\\mathbb S^2$ such that $$f=A\\left(a_0+\\frac{a_1}{r}+\\frac{a_2}{r^2}+\\mathcal E\\right).\n \\label{eq:projected-channel-expansion}$$ For each fixed angular differential operator $\\Omega^\\ell$, $$\\Omega^\\ell\\mathcal E=O(r^{-3}),\\qquad\n \\partial_r\\Omega^\\ell\\mathcal E=O(r^{-4}),\\qquad\n \\partial_r^2\\Omega^\\ell\\mathcal E=O(r^{-4}).\n \\label{eq:scalar-remainder-derivatives}$$ The radial remainders are odd in $\\omega_z$; in particular their bounds without angular differentiation gain a factor $|\\omega_z|$, also after one or two radial derivatives. All bounds are uniform for small $\\lambda$. Furthermore $a_0^\\lambda\\to a_0^0$ in $C^1(\\mathbb S^2)$, and phases can be chosen so that $$\\inf_{\\omega_z>0}\\frac{a_0^\\lambda(\\omega)}{\\omega_z}>c_D>0.\n \\label{eq:simple-angular-zero}$$ Finally, $$b=e^{-\\gamma r}r^{2/\\gamma-1}\n       \\bigl(b_\\infty(\\omega)+O(r^{-1})\\bigr),\\qquad\n \\min_{\\mathbb S^2}b_\\infty>0,\n \\label{eq:fast-orbital-expansion}$$ with angular derivatives and two radial derivatives of the expansion.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-plane-coefficient",
        "exact_quote": "There are smooth exponentially localized $Q\\mathcal K$-valued angular coefficients $T_2,T_3,T_4$, bounded uniformly for small $\\lambda$, such that $$QF=bG+\\lambda A\n       \\left(\\frac{T_2}{r^2}+\\frac{T_3}{r^3}+\\frac{T_4}{r^4}\\right)\n       +\\mathcal E_Q,\\qquad\n \\|\\partial_x^\\nu\\mathcal E_Q\\|\n       \\leq C_D\\lambda Ar^{-5}\\quad(|\\nu|\\leq2).\n \\label{eq:complementary-expansion}$$ Writing $Y_z=y_{1z}+y_{2z}$, one has $$\\begin{split}\n T_2&=-a_0B_Q^{-1}QW_2\\phi,\\\\\n T_2|_{\\omega_z=0}&=T_3|_{\\omega_z=0}=0,\\\\\n T_4|_{\\omega_z=0}\n   &=\\frac12B_Q^{-1}\\Delta_{\\mathbb S^2}T_2|_{\\omega_z=0}\n     =-a_0'(0)B_Q^{-2}QY_z\\phi .\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:equatorial-coefficient}$$ The last vector has norm bounded below by a positive constant at fixed $D$, uniformly for sufficiently small $\\lambda$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-fixed-parameter-channels",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $D$ satisfies [prop:two-centre-spectrum](#prop:two-centre-spectrum) and $$k_0>\\sqrt{0.98},\\qquad \\gamma<\\sqrt{49/45}.$$ Fix $t\\in(0,1]$. Assume the two-electron ground level is isolated and simple with an even ionic singlet, and the three-electron ground level in $S_z=1/2$ is isolated and simple, is an absolute ground level, and has a real axisymmetric odd ground state. Assume $n_t>0$, and set $$k=\\sqrt{2(E_2(t)-E_3(t))},\\qquad\n A_t(r)=e^{-kr}r^{(2-2t)/k-1}.$$ Suppose $$\\begin{gathered}\n 2\\epsilon_b-E_3(t)>0.49,\\qquad 0.99<k<\\gamma,\\\\\n \\inf_{\\omega_z>0}\\liminf_{r\\to\\infty}\n \\frac{\\|P_tF_{\\mathrm{true},t}(r\\omega)\\|}\n      {\\omega_z A_t(r)}>0.\n \\end{gathered}\n \\label{eq:fixed-channel-hypotheses}$$ Then $n_t$ has the local regularity in [lem:density-regularity](#lem:density-regularity), is smooth away from the nuclei, and satisfies [eq:plane-tail](#eq:plane-tail), [eq:plane-translate](#eq:plane-translate), [eq:bulk-translate](#eq:bulk-translate) at this fixed coupling, with $A=A_t$ and suitable positive constants. Membership of $t$ in the preceding perturbative interval is not required.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-angle-minimizer",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n$ be a positive, continuous, even density with $\\int n=3$, $n\\in H^2_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathbf R^3)$, and $\\sqrt n\\in H^1(\\mathbf R^3)$. The problem $$\\label{eq:angle-minimum}\n \\begin{aligned}\n &\\min_{\\vartheta\\in H^1(n;\\mathbf R)}\n       \\int_{\\mathbf R^3}n|\\nabla\\vartheta|^2,\\\\\n &\\text{subject to }\\quad\n       \\vartheta\\text{ odd},\\qquad\n       \\int_{\\mathbf R^3}n\\sin^2\\vartheta=1\n \\end{aligned}$$ has a minimizer $\\theta$ satisfying $0\\le\\theta\\le\\pi/2$ on $\\Omega_+$. Every such folded minimizer has a multiplier $d>0$ and satisfies $$\\label{eq:angle-euler}\n L_n\\theta:=n^{-1}\\operatorname{div}(n\\nabla\\theta)\n       =-d\\sin(2\\theta)\n \\quad\\hbox{on }\\mathbf R^3.$$ It belongs locally to $W^{2,p}$ for every $p<6$, and $$0<\\theta<\\pi/2\\quad\\hbox{on }\\Omega_+.$$ In particular, it is locally $C^{1,\\alpha}$ for every $0<\\alpha<1/2$. Axial symmetry of $n$ is not required for this proposition, and axial symmetry of $\\theta$ is not asserted.",
        "source_id": "PM-278.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-angle-minimizer",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n$ be a positive, continuous, even density with $\\int n=3$, $n\\in H^2_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathbf R^3)$, and $\\sqrt n\\in H^1(\\mathbf R^3)$. Every such folded minimizer has a multiplier $d>0$ and satisfies $$\\label{eq:angle-euler}\n L_n\\theta:=n^{-1}\\operatorname{div}(n\\nabla\\theta)\n       =-d\\sin(2\\theta)\n \\quad\\hbox{on }\\mathbf R^3.$$ It belongs locally to $W^{2,p}$ for every $p<6$, and $$0<\\theta<\\pi/2\\quad\\hbox{on }\\Omega_+.$$ In particular, it is locally $C^{1,\\alpha}$ for every $0<\\alpha<1/2$.         "exact_quote": "For every choice of folded minimizing angles for $n_\\lambda$, $$\\label{eq:angle-convergence}\n \\theta_\\lambda\\longrightarrow\n \\theta_*=\\arctan\\!\\left(\\frac{e}{\\sqrt2\\,b}\\right),\n \\qquad\n d_\\lambda\\longrightarrow d_*=\\epsilon_e-\\epsilon_b.$$ The angle convergence is weak in local $H^1$, strong in local $L^2$, and in $C^1$ on every fixed compact set.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-orbital-equations",
        "exact_quote": "For a folded minimizer define $$\\label{eq:angle-orbitals}\n g=\\sqrt n\\cos\\theta,\\qquad h=\\sqrt n\\sin\\theta.$$ Then $g,h\\in H^1(\\mathbf R^3)\\cap H^2_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathbf R^3)$, $g$ is even and strictly positive, and $h$ is odd and strictly positive on $\\Omega_+$. Moreover, $$\\|g\\|_2^2=2,\\qquad \\|h\\|_2^2=1,\\qquad\n \\langle g,h\\rangle=0,\\qquad g^2+h^2=n.$$ The real function $$\\label{eq:formal-potential}\n V=\\frac{\\Delta g}{2g}\n   =\\frac{\\Delta\\sqrt n}{2\\sqrt n}\n        -\\frac{|\\nabla\\theta|^2}{2}+d\\sin^2\\theta$$ belongs to $L^2_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathbf R^3)$, and $$\\label{eq:orbital-equations}\n (-\\Delta/2+V)g=0,\\qquad\n (-\\Delta/2+V)h=dh$$ in distributions. On the median plane, $g=\\sqrt n$ and $h=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-278.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-orbital-equations",
          "exact_quote": "For a folded minimizer define $$\\label{eq:angle-orbitals}\n g=\\sqrt n\\cos\\theta,\\qquad h=\\sqrt n\\sin\\theta.$$ Then $g,h\\in H^1(\\mathbf R^3)\\cap H^2_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathbf R^3)$, $g$ is even and strictly positive, and $h$ is odd and strictly positive on $\\Omega_+$. Moreover, $$\\|g\\|_2^2=2,\\qquad \\|h\\|_2^2=1,\\qquad\n \\langle g,h\\rangle=0,\\qquad g^2+h^2=n.$$ The real function $$\\label{eq:formal-potential}\n V=\\frac{\\Delta g}{2g}\n   =\\frac{\\Delta\\sqrt n}{2\\sqrt n}\n        -\\frac{|\\nabla\\theta|^2}{2}+d\\sin^2\\theta$$ belongs to $L^2_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathbf R^3)$, and $$\\label{eq:orbital-equations}\n (-\\Delta/2+V)g=0,\\qquad\n (-\\Delta/2+V)h=dh$$ in distributions. On the median plane, $g=\\sqrt n$ and $h=0$.",

        "exact_quote": "Suppose, for $p\\ge P_0$ and $0\\le z\\le L$, that $m(p,z)=e^{2kp}n(p,z)>0$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:strip-comparison-data}\n \\begin{aligned}\n |m_p|+|m_{pp}|&\\le\\eta m,\\qquad 0<\\eta\\le1,\\\\\n \\frac{m(p,t)}{m(p,s)}&\\le C\n       \\qquad(0\\le t\\le s\\le L).\n \\end{aligned}$$ Let $U(p,\\cdot)$ solve $$\\label{eq:strip-ode}\n n^{-1}(nU_z)_z=\\mu^2U,\\qquad U(p,0)=1,\\quad U(p,L)=0.$$ It is positive and decreasing on $0\\le z<L$, and $$\\label{eq:strip-horizontal-derivatives}\n |U_p|+|U_{pp}|\\le C_2\\eta U,$$ where $C_2$ depends only on $C,L,\\mu$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-quantitative-gap",
        "exact_quote": "Let $b,e,n_0,\\theta_*$ be the zero-coupling data for a fixed $D$ whose even gap satisfies $\\Delta_{\\mathrm{ev}}>0.2$. Let $n$ satisfy the hypotheses of [prop:angle-minimizer](#prop:angle-minimizer), be axisymmetric, and let $\\theta$ be a folded minimizer. Set $$\\eta_0=10^{-60},\\qquad M\\ge10^4,\\qquad S=100M.$$ Suppose $n$ is $C^2$ on a neighborhood of $\\{r\\ge M\\}$, $0.99<k<1.01$, $0<d<0.001$, and the following tests hold.*\n\n1.  *The two drift bounds of [eq:density-drift](#eq:density-drift) hold with $\\eta=\\eta_0$ for $r\\ge M$ on their stated domains. For $p\\ge M,\\ 0\\le z\\le L$, the bounds [eq:strip-comparison-data](#eq:strip-comparison-data) hold with $\\eta=\\eta_0$ and $C=100$.*\n\n2.  *Let $U,U_0$ be the strip solutions for $n,n_0$, and suppose $$\\alpha=\\inf_{0<z\\le L}\n         \\frac{(2/\\pi)\\delta_*(M,z)-U_0(M,z)}{z}>0,\n     \\qquad \\delta_*=\\pi/2-\\theta_*.$$ Uniformly on the cylinder $p=M,\\ 0\\le z\\le L$, including all azimuths for $\\theta$, one has $$|\\partial_z(\\theta-\\theta_*)|\n           +|\\partial_z(U-U_0)|<\\alpha/2.$$*\n\n3.  *On $r\\le2S$, the local weight comparison is $$\\frac12\\le\\frac{g^2}{2b^2}\\le2.$$*\n\n*Then holds for tests supported in $r>S$, and the full gap [eq:weighted-gap](#eq:weighted-gap) holds.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-ensemble-certificate",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the density regularity of [lem:density-regularity](#lem:density-regularity), the orbital hypotheses of [prop:orbital-equations](#prop:orbital-equations), and the weighted gap [eq:weighted-gap](#eq:weighted-gap). Then the formal potential $V$ satisfies $\\int_{\\mathbf R^3}n|V|<\\infty$. Every ensemble with density $n$ and finite kinetic energy obeys $$T[\\Gamma]+\\int_{\\mathbf R^3}Vn\\ge d.\n \\label{eq:ensemble-certificate}$$ The determinant $\\Phi_\\theta$ attains equality. Consequently, $$T_{\\mathrm{ens}}[n]\n =T[|\\Phi_\\theta\\rangle\\langle\\Phi_\\theta|]\n =d-\\int_{\\mathbf R^3}Vn.\n \\label{eq:ensemble-minimum}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-278.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-potential-identification",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the hypotheses of [prop:ensemble-certificate](#prop:ensemble-certificate). Suppose $H_v=\\sum_{i=1}^3(h_v\\otimes\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_{\\mathbf C^2})_i$ on $\\mathfrak H_3$ has a ground eigenspace supporting an ensemble $\\Gamma_s$ with $n_{\\Gamma_s}=n$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-translated-orbital-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "Write $V=V_{\\mathrm c}+V_{\\mathrm b}$, where $V_{\\mathrm c}\\in L^{3/2}(\\mathbf R^3)$ and $V_{\\mathrm b}\\in L^\\infty(\\mathbf R^3)$. If $|y_j|\\to\\infty$, then $$V_{\\mathrm c}(y_j+\\cdot)\\longrightarrow0\n \\quad\\text{in }L^{3/2}_{\\mathrm{loc}}(\\mathbf R^3).$$ After passing to a subsequence there is a real bounded function $W$ such that $$V_{\\mathrm b}(y_j+\\cdot)\\stackrel{*}{\\rightharpoonup}W\n \\quad\\text{locally in }L^\\infty,\n \\qquad \\|W\\|_\\infty\\le\\|V_{\\mathrm b}\\|_\\infty.$$ Suppose, in addition, that $u_j\\in H^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ solves $$\\left(-\\frac12\\Delta+V(y_j+\\cdot)\\right)u_j=\\varepsilon u_j$$ for one fixed real $\\varepsilon$, and that $u_j$ is bounded in $L^2$ on every compact set. A further subsequence converges strongly in $L^2_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ and weakly in $H^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ to a solution of $$\\left(-\\frac12\\Delta+W\\right)u=\\varepsilon u.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-278.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-translated-orbital-compactness",
          "exact_quote": "Write $V=V_{\\mathrm c}+V_{\\mathrm b}$, where $V_{\\mathrm c}\\in L^{3/2}(\\mathbf R^3)$ and $V_{\\mathrm b}\\in L^\\infty(\\mathbf R^3)$. A further subsequence converges strongly in $L^2_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ and weakly in $H^1_{\\mathrm{loc}}$ to a solution of $$\\left(-\\frac12\\Delta+W\\right)u=\\varepsilon u.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-278.1"

      "exact_statement": "Write $V=V_{\\mathrm c}+V_{\\mathrm b}$, where $V_{\\mathrm c}\\in L^{3/2}(\\mathbf R^3)$ and $V_{\\mathrm b}\\in L^\\infty(\\mathbf R^3)$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **potential space V** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We consider the usual three-dimensional potential space $$\mathcal V=L^{3/2}(\mathbf R^3;\mathbf R)+L^\infty(\mathbf R^3;\mathbf R).$$
- **noninteracting Hamiltonian H_s(v)** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For $v\in\mathcal V$, the noninteracting Hamiltonian $$H_s(v)=\sum_{i=1}^3\left(-\frac12\Delta_i+v(r_i)\right)$$ acts on the antisymmetric space $\bigwedge^3L^2(\mathbf R^3;\mathbf C^2)$, with the same scalar potential for both spin components.
- **ground-state ensemble** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A ground-state ensemble is a positive trace-class operator $\Gamma$ of trace one whose range is contained in the eigenspace at $\inf\sigma(H_s(v))$.
- **three-electron ensemble** ([source](#pm-h-21)): An ensemble is a positive trace-class operator $\Gamma$ on $\mathfrak H_3=\bigwedge^3\mathfrak h$, where $\mathfrak h=L^2(\mathbf R^3;\mathbf C^2)$, with $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\Gamma=1$.
- **paired orbitals g and h** ([source](#pm-h-4)): The orbitals $$g=\sqrt n\cos\theta,\qquad h=\sqrt n\sin\theta$$ have squared norms two and one, respectively.
- **regular auxiliary coupling** ([source](#stmt-def-regular-data)): An auxiliary coupling $t\in[0,1]$ is called regular if all the following conditions hold.
- **exact integer charge Z** ([source](#stmt-def-explicit-charge)): Using only the preceding data for the fixed unit-charge problem, define $$Z=2+\left\lceil \sup_{0<t\le1}\frac1t\,\mathbf1_{\mathcal B}(t) \right\rceil . \label{eq:explicit-charge}$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-separation-main | scaled half-separation D in the main theorem | = | $10^{200}$ | Fixed in the main theorem |
| q-separation-threshold | separation threshold for the two-centre spectrum | ≥ | $100$ | All D at least the threshold |
| q-splitting-bound | splitting of the two lowest levels | < | $\frac{100}{D}$ | D at least the threshold |
| q-decay-deviation | deviation of the decay rates from one | < | $\frac{200}{D}$ | D at least the threshold |
| q-orthogonal-gap | gap above the second level on the orthogonal complement | > | $0.2$ | D at least the threshold |
| q-slow-rate | slow channel decay rate k | > | $0.99$ | Large D, small coupling |
| q-strip-ratio | vertical density ratio in the strip | ≤ | $20$ | Large radius, small coupling |
| q-strip-height | strip height L | = | $10$ | Fixed |
| q-core-norm | norm of the core vector G | = | $\sqrt2$ | Zero coupling |
| q-strip-constant | strip derivative constant | < | $10^{30}$ | Ratio constant at most the stated value |
| q-exterior-gap | exterior weighted gap constant | > | $0.03$ | Large D, small coupling |
| q-drift-tolerance | drift tolerance in the exact-charge section | = | $10^{-60}$ | Fixed |
| q-energy-margin | ionization energy margin in regular data | > | $0.49$ | Coupling in the unit interval |
| q-multiplier-bound | multiplier d in the quantitative gap test | < | $0.001$ | Folded minimizing angle |
| q-partition-radius | partition radius S | = | $100M$ | Radius M at least the stated bound |
| q-density-trace | trace of the one-particle reduced density matrix | = | $3$ | All ensembles |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-scaling** [claim](#stmt-prop-scaling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-scaling): Unitary dilation of the Hamiltonian and density; scaling of the two pieces of the potential in the sum space. -> transfer of the obstruction to integer charges
- **s-two-centre** [claim](#stmt-prop-two-centre-spectrum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-two-centre-spectrum): Localization with a quadratic partition, hydrogenic bounds, trial functions and min-max; positivity by resolvent kernels. -> simple even and odd lowest levels and a gap
- **s-small-coupling** [claim](#stmt-prop-small-coupling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-small-coupling): Analytic perturbation of isolated rank-one spectral projections and comparison of spin sectors. -> isolated ground states for small repulsion
- **s-density-reg** [claim](#stmt-lem-density-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-density-regularity): Fubini in the distinguished coordinate, Sobolev estimates and product identities for the density. -> Sobolev regularity and compact convergence of the density
- **s-channel-cutoff** [claim](#stmt-lem-channel-cutoff) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-channel-cutoff): Exterior localization, exponential weights, cutoffs of the interaction and parallel-derivative estimates. -> cutoff channel equation with small residual
- **s-channel-expansion** [claim](#stmt-lem-channel-expansion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-channel-expansion): Supersolution comparison for the norm, angular induction and an integrating-factor recursion. -> slow-channel expansion with simple angular zero
- **s-plane-coefficient** [claim](#stmt-lem-plane-coefficient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-plane-coefficient): Recursion for the complementary channel with reduced ionic inverses and comparison for the remainder. -> nonzero equatorial coefficient of the complementary term
- **s-density-props** [claim](#stmt-prop-density-properties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-plane-coefficient-2): Three-scale lower bound, spin noncancellation and differentiated comparisons through the crossover. -> positivity, drift bounds, plane tail and translation limits
- **s-fixed-channels** [claim](#stmt-prop-fixed-parameter-channels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fixed-parameter-channels): Repeat the channel construction at fixed coupling with coupling-dependent constants. -> plane tail and translation limits at one coupling
- **s-angle-min** [claim](#stmt-prop-angle-minimizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-angle-minimizer): Folding, direct method, Lagrange multiplier, elliptic regularity bootstrap and Harnack inequality. -> folded minimizer with Euler equation
- **s-angle-conv** [claim](#stmt-prop-angle-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-angle-convergence): Zero-coupling identification of the minimizer, recovery sequence, compactness and multiplier formula. -> convergence of angles and multipliers
- **s-orbital** [claim](#stmt-prop-orbital-equations) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-orbital-equations): Sobolev chain rule and differentiation of the orbitals using the Euler equation. -> two orbitals with a common formal potential
- **s-strip** [claim](#stmt-lem-strip-solution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-strip-solution): Volterra series for the flux and termwise differentiation of the kernels. -> comparison function with controlled horizontal derivatives
- **s-ext-gap** [claim](#stmt-prop-exterior-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-exterior-gap): Subsolution comparison in the strip, ground-state transform, boundary flux cost and outward vector field. -> exterior weighted gap
- **s-weighted-gap** [claim](#stmt-prop-weighted-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-weighted-gap): Odd ground-state transform; even part by contradiction with localization and the unperturbed even gap. -> full weighted gap
- **s-quant-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-quantitative-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quantitative-gap): Finite tests give subsolution, boundary ordering and exterior gap; localization with a constant shift gives the even part. -> full weighted gap without compactness
- **s-ensemble-cert** [claim](#stmt-prop-ensemble-certificate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ensemble-certificate): Integrability of the formal potential, positive-orbital identity, Pauli bound and summation over natural orbitals. -> kinetic minimum attained by the paired determinant
- **s-potential-id** [claim](#stmt-prop-potential-identification) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-potential-identification): Form bounds, minimality of the determinant, square-root representation and variation of one orbital. -> representing potential equals the formal potential plus a constant
- **s-translated** [claim](#stmt-lem-translated-orbital-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-translated-orbital-compactness): Vanishing tails, weak-star compactness, cutoff energy estimate and Rellich compactness. -> limits of translated potentials and orbitals
- **s-potential-limits** [claim](#stmt-prop-potential-limits) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-potential-limits): Translate along the plane and away from it, use Harnack and maximum-principle arguments, and identify the limit equation. -> unique constant potential limit
- **s-ext-form** [claim](#stmt-lem-exterior-form-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exterior-form-limit): Uniform local form estimate by contradiction and a locally finite quadratic partition. -> exterior form coercivity
- **s-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-thm-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-obstruction): Exponentially weighted estimate of the first orbital, trace on the median plane, and divergence of the plane integral. -> formal potential outside the sum space and non-representability
- **s-qualitative** [claim](#stmt-thm-qualitative-counterexample) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-qualitative-counterexample): Choose large separation, then small coupling, apply the obstruction and scale to integer charges. -> family of counterexamples
- **s-param-radius** [claim](#stmt-lem-parameter-radius) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-parameter-radius): Small couplings are regular; uniform drift estimates and an explicit strip computation show the defining set is nonempty; comparison gives a positive margin. -> finite radius and positive margin
- **s-charge-finite** [claim](#stmt-lem-explicit-charge-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-explicit-charge-finite): Convergence for every folded minimizer shows that the bad set avoids small couplings. -> finite charge with regular reciprocal outside the bad set
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-explicit-charge-finite-2): Verify the fixed-parameter channel hypotheses and the quantitative gap at the selected coupling, then apply the obstruction and scaling. -> main non-representability theorem
- **s-hxc** [claim](#stmt-cor-hxc-first-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-hxc-first-variation): Attaining determinant at the physical density, subgradient argument, convexity of the kinetic search and ground-state variational principle. -> empty subdifferential and no full-domain first variation

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-ensemble-nonrepresentability**: For the specified finite two-nucleus, three-electron Coulomb molecule, the interacting ground-state density is not the density of any ground-state ensemble of noninteracting electrons in a single real, spin-independent potential from the sum space of the critical Lebesgue class and the bounded class.
- **inv-dilation-transfer**: A failure of ensemble representability for the scaled unit-charge problem with coupling equal to the reciprocal of an integer charge transfers to the physical molecule with that integer charge, by a unitary dilation.
- **inv-fixed-density-obstruction**: For a density, angle and orbitals with the stated analytic properties, plane tail and translation asymptotics and a weighted gap, the formal potential lies outside the sum space and no admissible ensemble represents the density.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-numerical-benchmark**: Use the specified molecule as a numerical test fixture for density-inversion or Kohn-Sham codes. -> The separation parameter is astronomically large and the charge is given only by a nonnumerical supremum formula, so no discretization or evaluated value is available.
- **anti-wider-potential-classes**: Cite the result as an obstruction for spin-dependent, distributional or nonlocal effective potentials. -> The exclusion concerns only a single real, spin-independent local potential from the stated sum space; larger classes are not covered.
- **anti-explicit-value-of-charge**: Read an explicit decimal value or bound for the charge off the definition. -> Finiteness of the supremum is proved, but the integer is not evaluated.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are analytic existence and nonexistence statements: a charge defined as a supremum over a set of couplings whose membership depends on spectral and variational minimizers of an infinite-dimensional problem, with no effective numerical bound asserted, and an obstruction proved by weighted trace arguments. No finite procedure computes an object here, so no runnable reference and oracle exist.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is an existence and non-representability theorem for one fixed Coulomb molecule. It gives no finite procedure that code could run, and its parameters are far outside any practical numerical regime. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The dilation to integer nuclear charges is an analytic equivalence of Hamiltonians. It is not a simulation scheme, and nothing in it supports real-time or physics-engine use. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only tool-like consequence is negative: no local first variation exists at the physical density. It supplies no tool construction, and its scope is limited to the stated potential class. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The integer charge is defined by a supremum over auxiliary couplings, each requiring spectral and variational tests. The paper states that this is not an effective procedure and asserts no numerical bound, so no algorithm with a complexity can be extracted. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The antisymmetric many-electron spaces are abstract Hilbert spaces used in the analysis. They suggest no concrete representation or data structure. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper explicitly declines to give any evaluated numerical value for the charge, so there is no kernel or workload for an accelerator to run. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A fixed-density obstruction in quantum mechanics has no hardness assumption, adversary model or security notion attached to it. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a limit on exact density-to-potential inversion for one single-potential class. It is a specification boundary for learned functionals or inverters, not a learning construction, and it says nothing about other model classes. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An angle minimization appears, but only as an analytic device to certify a kinetic-energy minimum. Existence is proved variationally, with no solver, rate or feasible-set algorithm. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The limits taken along escaping translations are mathematical compactness arguments. They involve no nodes, messages, concurrency or fault model. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Its sole use is to delimit what the theorem excludes. It concerns a nonexistence statement in mathematical density-functional theory, with no construction to transfer. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-potential-class** (formal_scope): The exclusion concerns only a single real, spin-independent local potential in the sum space. It says nothing about wider classes of potentials or about nonlocal operators. Blocked use: ['Citing the theorem as an obstruction for spin-dependent potentials', 'Citing the theorem as an obstruction for distributional potentials', 'Citing the theorem as an obstruction for nonlocal one-particle operators']
- **limit-nonnumerical-charge** (nonconstructive): The charge is defined through a supremum over auxiliary couplings and no evaluated value or effective bound is given. The integer is known to be finite but cannot be computed from the paper. Blocked use: ['Reading a decimal value or numerical upper bound for the charge off the definition', 'Using the molecule as a numerical benchmark or regression fixture']
- **limit-single-fixed-system** (edge_case): The main theorem concerns one fixed finite system with a specific separation parameter. It does not describe generic molecules or other electron numbers. Blocked use: ['Extrapolating non-representability to arbitrary molecules', 'Extrapolating the result to systems with a different number of electrons']
- **limit-large-separation** (hypothesis): The two-centre spectral facts (simple lowest levels, a gap above them) are proved only for large separation parameter. The perturbative analysis builds on them. Blocked use: ['Applying the spectral gap statements to small separation', 'Applying the small-coupling construction to small separation']
- **limit-small-coupling-interval** (hypothesis): The isolated simple ground states are asserted only for sufficiently small repulsion coupling, and the length of that interval depends on the separation parameter. Blocked use: ['Using the perturbative ground-state branch at moderate coupling', 'Assuming a coupling interval uniform in the separation parameter']
- **limit-angle-symmetry** (edge_case): The minimizing angle is not claimed to be axisymmetric, even when the density is. Arguments must hold for every folded minimizer and all azimuths. Blocked use: ['Reducing the angle problem to two variables', 'Assuming a unique or axisymmetric minimizer']
- **limit-quantitative-gap-tests** (hypothesis): The finite-test criterion for the full weighted gap needs the stated spectral window, the multiplier bound and the drift, strip, boundary and weight tests simultaneously. Failure of any one test voids the conclusion. Blocked use: ['Certifying the gap after checking only some of the tests', 'Applying the criterion outside the stated window for the decay rate']
- **limit-fixed-parameter-obstruction** (formal_scope): The fixed-density obstruction needs the plane tail, both translation limits and the weighted gap at the one chosen parameter. It does not hold for a density lacking them. Blocked use: ['Applying the obstruction to a density without the plane tail', 'Applying the obstruction without verifying the weighted gap']
- **limit-family-threshold** (approximation): The family result gives a threshold on the charge that depends on the separation parameter, and the threshold is not quantified. It does not say which charges lie beyond it. Blocked use: ['Assuming a particular small integer charge belongs to the family', 'Reading an explicit threshold from the existence statement']
- **limit-functional-variation** (counterexample_scope): The functional consequence excludes a potential in the sum space giving the one-sided variation along every segment in the finite-energy domain. It does not exclude variations along restricted directions or with distributional potentials. Blocked use: ['Concluding that no functional derivative exists in any sense', 'Concluding that approximate exchange-correlation potentials are impossible']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a finite three-electron Coulomb molecule whose spin-summed ground-state density cannot be reproduced by any ground-state ensemble of noninteracting electrons in a single real, spin-independent local potential in $`L^{3/2}(\mathbf R^3)+L^\infty(\mathbf R^3)`$. The molecule has two equal positive integer nuclear charges, specified by an exact finite nonnumerical formula.
