Also $0.99<k_{t_*}<\gamma<1.01$ and $M>10D$. The hypotheses of [lem:quantitative-gap](#lem:quantitative-gap) are therefore satisfied. To indicate the numerical margins, its strip derivative constant is below $10^{30}$, so its horizontal error is at most $4\cdot10^{-30}U$, whereas $\mu^2-2d>0.008$. At the inner cylinder, the reference gap is at least $\alpha z$; the derivative test reduces it by less than $\alpha z/2$. The exterior gap is $0.03$, while the localization error $10/S^2$ leaves a gap far larger than $d<0.001$. Thus the full weighted gap holds for the constructed orbitals $g,h$.

The fixed-density obstruction in [thm:obstruction](#thm:obstruction), using the ensemble certificate and potential identification from [sec:ensemble](#sec:ensemble), rules out every representing potential in the stated class for this scaled density. Finally, [prop:scaling](#prop:scaling) transfers the obstruction to the physical system with charges $Z,Z$. The number $Z$ is finite, so the nuclear positions $(0,0,\pm D/Z)$ are finite and distinct. This establishes [thm:main](#thm:main). ◻

</details>

All quantities in the prescription are determined by the fixed unit-charge auxiliary operators and their density-angle minimizations. Eigenvector phases do not enter, and the tests quantify over every minimizing angle. The supremum in [eq:explicit-charge](#eq:explicit-charge) is therefore an unambiguous definition of an integer; it contains no test for representability by an unknown potential. Its nonnumerical character concerns the evaluation of that integer, not the finiteness of the system or the order of limits in the proof.

<a id="sec:hxc-first-variation"></a>
### A functional consequence for the physical density

In this section all coordinates and densities are physical, as in [thm:main](#thm:main). Work on the real Banach space $X=L^1(\mathbf R^3;\mathbf R)\cap L^3(\mathbf R^3;\mathbf R)$ with the sum norm and its potential dual $\mathcal V$, paired by integration. In addition to the kinetic search <a href="#eq:ensemble-search" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ensemble-search">[eq:ensemble-search]</a>, define $$F_{\mathrm{ens}}[m]
 =\inf_{\Gamma%
  \BeginAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct,method=hex,unicode,ActualText=21A6}%
  \KSOriginalMapsto\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}m}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}((T+W)\Gamma),
 \qquad
 T=\sum_{i=1}^3-\frac12\Delta_i,
 \quad W=\sum_{i<j}\frac1{|r_i-r_j|}.$$ Both searches run over all positive trace-one ensembles on $\mathfrak H_3$, with form expectations, and take value $+\infty$ when there is no finite-energy competitor. Their common finite-energy domain and ensemble Hartree–exchange–correlation functional are $$\mathcal D=\mathop{\mathrm{Dom}}T_{\mathrm{ens}}=\mathop{\mathrm{Dom}}F_{\mathrm{ens}},
 \qquad
 E_{\mathrm{Hxc}}=F_{\mathrm{ens}}-T_{\mathrm{ens}}
 \quad\text{on }\mathcal D.$$ Indeed, the Coulomb Hardy estimate from [sec:setup](#sec:setup) gives $0\le W\le\varepsilon T+C_\varepsilon$ as quadratic forms, also after taking ensemble expectations. Thus finite kinetic and interacting internal energies are equivalent. Such densities belong to $X$: <a href="#eq:ensemble-density" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ensemble-density">[eq:ensemble-density]</a> and Cauchy–Schwarz give $\int|\nabla\sqrt{n_\Gamma}|^2\le2T[\Gamma]$, and Sobolev together with $\int n_\Gamma=3$ gives the assertion. Mixtures and approximate minimizers show that both searches are convex, so $\mathcal D$ is convex. For either search $G$, write $$\partial_{\mathcal V}G[n]
 =\left\{u\in\mathcal V:
 G[m]\ge G[n]+\int u(m-n)\ \text{for every }m\in X\right\}.$$

<a id="stmt-cor-hxc-first-variation"></a>
<div id="cor:hxc-first-variation" class="corollary">

<a id="eq:hxc-full-segment-variation"></a>
**Corollary 29** (Absence of a full-domain local first variation). *For the physical ground-state density $n=n_\Psi$ of [thm:main](#thm:main), $\partial_{\mathcal V}T_{\mathrm{ens}}[n]=\varnothing$. Moreover, there is no $w\in\mathcal V$ such that, for every $m\in\mathcal D$, $$\lim_{t\downarrow0}
 \frac{E_{\mathrm{Hxc}}[n+t(m-n)]-E_{\mathrm{Hxc}}[n]}{t}
 =\int_{\mathbf R^3}w(m-n).
 \label{eq:hxc-full-segment-variation}$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-hxc-first-variation"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-hxc-first-variation</summary>

*Proof.* Proposition <a href="#prop:ensemble-certificate" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:ensemble-certificate">18</a> supplies an attaining determinant for the scaled kinetic search. The inverse dilation in Proposition <a href="#prop:scaling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:scaling">2</a> gives a determinant $\Phi_{\rm phys}$ attaining $T_{\mathrm{ens}}[n]$: it is a bijection of ensembles with the corresponding densities and multiplies kinetic energies by $Z^2$. If $u\in\partial_{\mathcal V}T_{\mathrm{ens}}[n]$, the subgradient inequality makes $\Phi_{\rm phys}$ a minimizer of the form of $H_s(-u)$ over all normalized antisymmetric states in its form domain. The form bound <a href="#eq:ensemble-standard-form-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ensemble-standard-form-bound">[eq:ensemble-standard-form-bound]</a> applies to $u$, so equality at the bottom of this closed form makes $\Phi_{\rm phys}$ a ground eigenstate, as in the proof of Proposition <a href="#prop:potential-identification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:potential-identification">19</a>. Its density is $n$, contradicting [thm:main](#thm:main).

Let $v_{\rm ext}\in\mathcal V$ be the physical nuclear potential in $H_C$. The ground-state variational principle gives $-v_{\rm ext}\in\partial_{\mathcal V}F_{\mathrm{ens}}[n]$; in particular, $\Psi$ attains the interacting search at $n$. Suppose now that $w$ satisfies <a href="#eq:hxc-full-segment-variation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:hxc-full-segment-variation">[eq:hxc-full-segment-variation]</a>. Fix $m\in\mathcal D$, set $h=m-n$ and $n_t=n+th$. Convexity of the kinetic search and the interacting support inequality give, for $0<t\le1$, $$\begin{aligned}
 T_{\mathrm{ens}}[m]-T_{\mathrm{ens}}[n]
 &\ge\frac{T_{\mathrm{ens}}[n_t]-T_{\mathrm{ens}}[n]}{t}\\
 &\ge-\int v_{\rm ext}h
       -\frac{E_{\mathrm{Hxc}}[n_t]-E_{\mathrm{Hxc}}[n]}{t}.
\end{aligned}$$ Letting $t\downarrow0$ proves the subgradient inequality for $-v_{\rm ext}-w$ and every $m\in\mathcal D$. Outside $\mathcal D$ it is automatic because $T_{\mathrm{ens}}=+\infty$. Hence $-v_{\rm ext}-w\in\partial_{\mathcal V}T_{\mathrm{ens}}[n]$, contrary to the first assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-31"></a>
#### References

<div class="thebibliography">

10

<a id="ref-Corso26"></a>T. C. Corso, A rigorous formulation of density functional theory for spinless fermions in one dimension, *Letters in Mathematical Physics* **116** (2026), article 27. [doi:10.1007/s11005-026-02051-1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s11005-026-02051-1).

<a id="ref-ETG25"></a>J. Erhard, E. Trushin, and A. Görling, Kohn–Sham inversion for open-shell systems, *The Journal of Chemical Physics* **162** (2025), 034116. [doi:10.1063/5.0239422](https://doi.org/10.1063/5.0239422).

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "A meridional section of the scaled molecule, drawn schematically and not to scale. The nuclei lie at $± De_{z}$; the horizontal line is the median plane $z = 0$. A bounded window is translated to a distant point $pω$ in that plane. After normalization by $(A(p)/p)^{2}$, the limiting density in the window vanishes quadratically on the plane, although the actual density there has a smaller nonzero tail. This distinction drives the limiting-orbital and trace arguments.",
      "figure_id": "fig:median-plane",
      "pdf_page": 3,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:median-plane",
          "exact_quote": "A meridional section of the scaled molecule, drawn\nschematically and not to scale. The nuclei lie at \\(\\pm De_z\\);\nthe horizontal line is the median plane \\(z=0\\). A bounded window\nis translated to a distant point \\(p\\omega\\) in that plane.",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[x=1cm,y=1cm,>=Latex,font=\\small]\n  \\fill[black!4] (-1,-0.08) rectangle (10,0.08);\n  \\draw[black!55] (-1,0) -- (10,0);\n  \\draw[->] (0,-1.9) -- (0,2.1) node[above] {$z$};\n  \\fill (0,1.25) circle (2pt) node[left=7pt] {$+De_z$};\n  \\fill (0,-1.25) circle (2pt) node[left=7pt] {$-De_z$};\n  \\node[below right] at (0,0) {$0$};\n  \\node[below] at (3.3,-0.12) {median plane};\n  \\draw[->,thick] (1.1,0.5) -- (5.2,0.5)\n    node[midway,above] {$p\\longrightarrow\\infty$};\n  \\draw[dashed,black!60] (6.3,-1.35) rectangle (9.3,1.35);\n  \\fill (7.1,0) circle (1.7pt) node[below=5pt] {$p\\omega$};\n  \\draw[->] (7.1,0) -- (8.55,0) node[right] {$\\omega$};\n  \\draw[->] (7.1,0) -- (7.1,0.9) node[above] {$z$};\n  \\node[above] at (7.8,1.43) {translated window};\n  \\node[below] at (7.8,-1.45) {$\\sqrt{n(p,0)}\\gtrsim\\lambda A(p)p^{-4}$};\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A schematic meridional cross-section diagram showing a vertical $z$-axis with nuclei marked by black dots at $+De_z$ and $-De_z$, and the origin labeled $0$. A horizontal line represents the median plane $z = 0$. A horizontal arrow pointing to the right along the median plane is labeled $p \\longrightarrow \\infty$. At a distant point $p\\omega$ on the median plane, a dashed rectangular box labeled \"translated window\" is shown with local axes $z$ (vertical) and $\\omega$ (horizontal). Beneath the translated window is the inequality $\\sqrt{n(p, 0)} \\gtrsim \\lambda A(p)p^{-4}$."
    }
  ],
